BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
BOARD MEETING   MINU TES OCTOBER 19, 2010 
    SERVICES BUILDING  
 President Underwood called the meeting to order at 7:02 
p.m. 
In addition to Ms. Underwood, present were Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, and 
Philippart. Mrs. Pochodylo was absent. Two student 
representatives were in attendance.  Also present were 
Superintendent Fowler; administrators Birmingham, 
Keeling, Kwasny, Machesky, Mull, Papasian, Rajter, 
Seppanen, and Witt and; 45 students from Martell 
Elementary School, and approximately 65 additional 
members of the audience.   
RECOGNITION 
that Troy Families for Safe Homes and the Troy Coalition 
sponsored this contest encouraging students to create 
their own songs about staying Drug, Alcohol and Tobacco 
free.  The Martell singers and their teacher, Mrs. Andrea 
Moon, composed an original song called, “Don’t Let Drugs 
Get in the Way of Your Dreams.”  The students sang their 
song for the Board and were applauded and received a 
standing ovation.  Ms. Underwood and Mr. Miletti 
presented each child with a certificate and pin. 
Ms. Emily Jambeau, vocal music teacher at Bemis 
Elementary, informed the Board of a new district-wide 
Fourth Grade Hit Song Contest using Popular Music as 
the theme.  Fourth grade students interested in 
participating must create lyrics to a song either on their 
own or in a small group.  This creative activity will allow 
students to develop a higher-level of thinking and also 
give them the opportunity to perform at a real festival.  A 
video clip was shared explaining the contest and rules. 
Two winners will be chosen and will perform their songs 
in the 2011 Festival of Songs Concert in January.    
Ms. Underwood commented on what a fantastic 
beginning the students’ performance provided to tonight’s 
Board Meeting and wished they could sing before every 
meeting. 
7

Mrs. Keeling announced Marte ll fifth grade students who 
won a song writing contest la st year, which they created 
in honor of Red Ribbon Week.  Mrs. Keeling explained 
Mr. Hauff added that the children were better than 
“America’s Got Talent”.   
Ms. Philippart stated that she was looking forward to 
hearing news on the Hit Contest as well.

Whereas, student achievement is honored through the 
National Merit Scholarship semifinalist and finalist 
recognition programs; and 
Whereas, National Merit students reached semifinalist 
status by scoring in the top one and one-half percent on 
the National Preliminary Scholastic Aptitude Test (PSAT) 
and earning comparable scores on the Scholastic Aptitude 
Test (SAT). 
Now Therefore Be It Resolved  that the Troy School 
District Board of Education adopts this resolution in honor 
of the aforementioned Troy School District students for 
their selection as 2010-2011 National Merit Scholarship 
Semifinalists.

APT to Succeed program, its partnership with TBERT 
(Troy Business and Educat ion Roundtable) and the 
positive impact it has had on our students.  He 
introduced Mrs. Jennifer Tayl or, teacher at Boulan Park 
Middle School, who shared a presentation with the Board. 
Mrs. Taylor emphasized the importance of this program 
and the need for continued support of volunteers to 
ensure its success.  She prov ided a brief history on how 
the program was started by Troy Chamber of Commerce 
and Larson Middle School.  She reviewed the various 
functions that teachers, parents and the Troy Chamber 
can do to assist with this program and again encouraged 
the community to get involved.   
commitment to this program.  The Chair of TBERT is 
committed to supporting the district in soliciting

Mr. Miletti shared that he has participated in this program 
for the last three to four years and encouraged others to 
volunteer their time for this engaging experience.  He 
stated that the communicati on skills of the eighth 
graders’ participating in this program surpass those of an 
eighth grade level.  He remarked how this is a great 
example of student achievement and should be 
recognized and celebrated.

Andrew Michail wished the freshmen at Troy High good 
produce quality as well as the different cultures 
associated with those foods.  He also reminded everyone 
that Troy vs. Athens footba ll game is this Friday and 
finished with “Go Colts!”     
Bria Ellis reported that th e Athens PTO had their third 
Drive One Fundraiser, which raised $4,200 this year and 
an overall total of $13,300, which will be put towards 
student scholarships.  She thanked everyone who came 
out and supported the PTO and students.  She also 
wished the freshman at Athens good luck as they take 
the MEAP test tomorrow.  She invited the Board and 
community to participate in their Red Cross Blood Drive 
year’s goal is to collect 76 pints of blood, which means 
113 people are needed to donate blood.  Bria was excited 
to share that Athens is hosting Summuna, a Model UN 
520 delegates called “Students Across Michigan” will be 
participating in this UN simulation.  State Senator 
Pappageorge is the key note speaker for the opening 
ceremony.   
Mrs. Edmunds reminded everyone about the upcoming 
of voting on Proposal 1 to keep the Troy Public Library 
open.  She stated that if Proposal 1 does not pass the 
library will close its doors on June 30 th and negatively 
impact the large numbers of district students who use the 
library. She urged the community to exercise their right 
to vote and to vote yes on Proposal 1.

 RE: Water Treatment Bid #9695 
Troy, MI 48083 
248/823-4050 
We recently requested bids for the Heating and Cooling systems water treatment for th 2010, 
2011 and 2012 school years. There were three bidders. I have tabulated the 3year totals on the 
bids and they are as follows: 
Enerco Corporation -   2010- $20,835.00, 2011- $29,630.00, 2012- $29,900.00, Total $80,365.00
H.V. Burton Co.-          2010- $28,439.00, 2011- $28,439.00, 2012- $29,844.00,  Total $86,722.00 
D & L Water Control-  2010- $76,563.50, 2011- $76,563.50, 2012- $76,563.50,  Total $229,690.50   
Based on the bids received it is my recommendation that the Water Treatment Bid be 
awarded to Enerco Corporation in the amount of $80,365.00 divided out over the three year 
period as outlined above. 
Note: For clarity please be advise that the 2010 bid covers the period from November 1,2010 
thru June 30, 2011 and the 2011 bid covers the period July 1, 2011 thru June 30, 2012 and the 
2012 bid covers the period July1, 2012 thru June 30, 2013. This is also clearly stated in the bid 
documents. 
If you have any questions, please feel free to contact me.

                      Water Treatment Chemicals                      
On October 19, 2010 bids were opened for water treatment chemicals to be used 
throughout the District over a three year period.   Bids were sent to nine potential 
bidders with three responding (none of which were “No Bid”).   
Results of this bid have been reviewed with Mr. M. Belardi, Director of Maintenance, 
who has recommended award to Enerco Corporation.  I have also reviewed this bid and 
concur with this recommendation.  Accordingly, I recommend award of this bid as 
follows:

expenditure of the 2010-11, 2011-12 and 2012-13 General Fund.

7. A.        11.16.10 
7. CURRICULUM 
 A. CONSIDERATION:  Great Start Readiness Grant Acceptance  
 The Troy School District is eligible to receive funds from the Michigan Department of 
Education to provide educational services for at-risk four-year-old preschoolers.  To accept the 
funds, the Board is required to approve a resolution certifying that the Troy School District will 
participate in the 2010-2011 Great Start Readiness State Aid program and to indicate the type 
of services we will provide. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:   Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
   Moved by:                 
           Supported by:          
WHEREAS, the Legislature continues to acknow ledge the importance of improving school 
readiness by providing quality preschool educat ion for four-year-old children who may have 
extraordinary need of special assistance and the importance of providing parenting education 
programs for families of all children, birth to kindergarten entry; and 
WHEREAS, Sections 32d and 37 through 40 of the State School Aid Act provide funding for 
comprehensive compensatory education for these children and families; and 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the provisions of  Section 38 of the State School Aid Act, Troy 
School District is eligible to receive an allocation of $108,800 for fiscal year 2010-2011 to 
operate a school readiness program for 32 ch ildren and/or to provide comprehensive parent 
involvement and education programs for all families of children, birth to kindergarten entry; 
now therefore be it 
RESOLVED that Troy School District certifie s that it will not provide full-day preschool 
education for eligible four-year-old children and that $ -0- of the stated amount, based on the 
approved per-capita allowance of $6,600 will be used specifically for this purpose; and 
therefore be it 
RESOLVED that Troy School District certifies that it will not provide comprehensive parent 
involvement and education programs for all families of children, birth to kindergarten entry, 
and that $ -0- will be used specifically for this purpose; now there be it resolved 
108
RESOLVED that Troy School District certifies th at it will provide half-day preschool education 
for 32 eligible four-year-old children and that $108,800 of the stated amount, based on the 
approved per-capita allowance of $3,400, will be specifically for this purpose; and 
Furthermore, the Troy School District certifies that more than fifty percent of the children 
enrolled in the school readiness program will meet the income eligibility guidelines outlined in 
Sections 32d and 37 through 40; and be it finally 
RESOLVED that any unallocated funds, as a result  of this resolution, shall be redistributed to 
other eligible districts. 
Ayes:              
Nays:              
109
Adopted this 16
th day of November 2010. 
     8.A. 11.16.10  
8. BUSINESS  
A. REPORT:   Budget Forecast 
Like all Michigan school distri cts, Troy has been struggling to 
balance its annual budget in the face of flat or insufficient revenue 
growth from the State of Michigan . The Board has been involved in 
several reviews of our district’s budget picture since the beginning 
of this school year. Tonight, Mark Rajter, Assistant Superintendent, 
Business Services, will present an updated five-year forecast. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
This report will be given by Mark Rajter.  No action is required.

   Supported by
